CHK-010 — Vulnerability, Support Needs and Foreseeable Harm Overlay

Outcome: Potential Concern

 

  No trigger was recorded; this check applies to every case and was assessed anyway. Potential Concern across 5 requirement(s). 5 raised a concern (G10.1, G10.2, G10.3, G10.4, G10.5). The report asserts third-party support is absent, but the file contains standard clauses permitting Power of Attorney arrangements without evidence of whether these were actually discussed, offered, or put in place for this specific client. The Fact Find records 'Preferred delivery method: No Preference' but provides no positive evidence of what communication methods or adjustments were actually implemented or offered. Risk profile assessment notes state 'No additional notes were recorded' for Investor Experience and Capacity for loss, creating ambiguity about whether vulnerability or support needs were assessed at all versus assessed and found absent. 7 cited quote(s) could not be found in the evidence supplied and were rejected.

 

  [G10.1] Details of additional support and needs, including third-party support

  Potential Concern — severity High — citations partly verified

    Report:   The suitability report asserts that third-party support is absent (isPresent: false). No name, relationship, or authority type is recorded. The extracted model shows no data under /vulnerability/perClient[]/supportNeeds[] and the third-party support fields are all null or absent.

    File:     The case file contains extensive information across multiple categories. Category A documents (TFAS Wealth Client Agreement Privacy.md, IMPORT_2.md) reference the firm's ability to accept Power of Attorney and third-party instructions: 'You may also allow a third party to provide instructions on your behalf by providing a valid Power of Attorney' [P17]. The Fact Find (Test 1).md [P7, P21] records 'Preferred delivery method: No Preference' with 'No additional notes were recorded' under various sections. Risk profile report.md [P5] states 'No additional notes were recorded' under Investor Experience and Capacity for loss questionnaire sections. The client declaration [P7] shows the client signed alone with no indication of support persons present. No vulnerability indicators, support needs, communication adjustments, or third-party representatives are explicitly recorded in any assessment document.

    Discrepancies:

      - The report asserts third-party support is absent, but the file contains standard clauses permitting Power of Attorney arrangements without evidence of whether these were actually discussed, offered, or put in place for this specific client.

      - The Fact Find records 'Preferred delivery method: No Preference' but provides no positive evidence of what communication methods or adjustments were actually implemented or offered.

      - Risk profile assessment notes state 'No additional notes were recorded' for Investor Experience and Capacity for loss, creating ambiguity about whether vulnerability or support needs were assessed at all versus assessed and found absent.

      - The file records no explicit vulnerability assessment outcome (present or absent) in the Risk Profile or Fact Find, only the absence of recorded discussion notes.

    NOT ASSESSED — the comparison needs a value that was not available: Evidence of whether a vulnerability assessment was conducted at all (the absence of notes may indicate no assessment occurred, or assessment occurred but no concerning findings were recorded), Confirmation of whether support needs, communication preferences, or adjustments were explicitly discussed and documented as not required, versus never being addressed, Records of any Power of Attorney discussion, offer, or arrangement for this specific client, Explicit confirmation that the client does not have vulnerabilities or support needs, rather than silence on the topic, Documentation of the adviser's professional judgment on capacity to understand, resilience to loss, or foreseeable harm considerations under PRIN 2A.5 and FCA FG21/1

    The core issue is ambiguity about assessment versus absence of findings. The report states third-party support is absent (isPresent: false), which is a positive assertion that it was considered and not found. However, the file does not provide evidence that vulnerability and support needs were systematically assessed. The Risk Profile shows questionnaire responses on investment knowledge ('No understanding / knowledge' for stocks, EIS/VCTs, and pension planning) [P1], indicating low capability in key areas, yet the notes section records 'No additional notes were recorded' [P5, P9]. This is a FG21/1 driver — low capability — yet there is no documented consideration of support needs or communication adjustments to compensate. The Fact Find records 'Preferred delivery method: No Preference' [P7] but does not establish what preference was offered, what adjustments were discussed, or whether the adviser considered whether the client's admitted lack of understanding required simplified communication, glossaries, or third-party support. The file permits Power of Attorney [P17] but does not record whether this was discussed with the client. The standard clauses in the Client Agreement are boilerplate; they do not evidence that support arrangements were assessed for this client's specific circumstances. Under the guards provided, a spouse present would not automatically indicate support; no family members are recorded as present. No Power of Attorney recommendation appears in the suitability report (it is future planning, not current support). The comparison cannot establish whether the report's assertion of no third-party support is correct because the file lacks positive documentation of a completed vulnerability and support needs assessment. Silence in the notes could mean assessment occurred and found nothing, or assessment never occurred. The report should have explained the outcome of its assessment and what support options were considered and why they were not needed; absence of such reasoning leaves the assertion unsupported.

  [G10.3] Adjustments made to support client comprehension

  Potential Concern — severity Moderate — citations partly verified

    Report:   The suitability report contains no assertion, statement or discussion of adjustments made to support client comprehension. The canonical model holds no value at /vulnerability/perClient[]/adjustmentsMade[].

    File:     The case file contains scattered references to communication and support arrangements but no systematic record of adjustments made specifically to support client comprehension. P2 records 'No Preference' for delivery method and generic marketing preferences. P1 states 'If there are any points on which you are unsure, or require further clarification, please contact me and I will be pleased to explain these in greater detail' — a standing offer to clarify, not an adjustment made. P5 and P11 discuss risk assessment and capacity for loss at length, confirming client understanding ('You are happy to proceed', 'You confirmed that you wish to increase'), but do not describe adjustments implemented to achieve that understanding. P4 references ongoing review and access to advisers, and P17 outlines the Client Agreement service terms, but neither describes adjustments to comprehension support. The file notes (P11, P14, P15) document adviser reasoning and alternative option analysis but contain no record of adjustments made to help the client understand the advice.

    Discrepancies:

      - Report is silent on adjustments made to support client comprehension; file contains confirmations that client understood key concepts (risk, capacity for loss, consolidation rationale) but no record of what adjustments or measures were implemented to achieve that understanding.

    NOT ASSESSED — the comparison needs a value that was not available: Systematic record of adjustments made to support client comprehension; communication preferences or accessible format requirements beyond 'No Preference' noted in P2; evidence of steps taken to ensure client could access, understand, or retain advice content; any reference to client vulnerabilities, support needs, or third-party involvement that would trigger tailored comprehension measures.

    The requirement asks whether information relating to client support needs, communication preferences, capacity, comprehension requirements or duty of care considerations is fully supported by evidence. The canonical model is empty at the relevant paths (/vulnerability/perClient[]/adjustmentsMade[], /vulnerability/perClient[]/characteristics[]), indicating either the report contains no such information or extraction failed to capture it. The extraction metadata states 'NotPresentInDocument' for neither path — both are simply absent. The comparison cannot be performed because the report asserts nothing about adjustments made to comprehension, and the file contains confirmations of understanding but no record of how that understanding was supported. The case file shows the client completed a risk questionnaire, discussions occurred, and understanding was confirmed, but it does not evidence what adjustments (if any) were made to support comprehension — no reference to preferred communication methods beyond 'No Preference', no accessible formats, no literacy considerations, no use of plain language versions, no third-party involvement, and no tailored support recorded. The absence of a trigger finding does not excuse the report from reflecting support needs if they exist in the file. The file contains no positive indicators of vulnerabilities, communication barriers, or support needs that would require adjustments, nor does it record any adjustments made. Without such indicators in the file, a finding of 'No Issue' would require the report to have addressed the question and concluded no adjustments were needed; instead, the report is silent. This silence in the context of a comprehensive suitability report reviewing financial affairs, risk, capacity, and ongoing service is a gap. Compliance with PRIN 2A.2.1R (fairness), PRIN 2A.5 (understanding), and PRIN 2A.6 (adequate supporting processes) requires that where a firm advises a consumer, it demonstrates care has been taken to ensure comprehension. A report that does not articulate what steps were taken, and a file that does not record them, leaves material doubt about whether the duty was discharged.

  [G10.4] Vulnerability has a material impact on the recommendation (overlay)

  Potential Concern — severity High

    Report:   The report identifies six foreseeable harms: (1) market volatility and fund fluctuations, mitigated by advice that funds may go up or down and multi-asset fund design; (2) underperformance of new funds, mitigated by performance analysis and critical yield assessment showing expected outperformance at 2.94% mid-growth; (3) pension switching costs reducing fund value, mitigated by no exit penalties identified and charge comparison provided; (4) illiquidity and administration delays, mitigated by client's £6,000 liquid cash reserves and 10-15 working day access timeline; (5) loss of employer contributions if People's Pension is left, mitigated by retention of People's Pension scheme; (6) Inheritance Tax impact from April 2027, mitigated by IHT analysis and position to be monitored. The report documents attitude to risk (rating 5 Balanced, upgraded from questionnaire rating 4) and capacity for loss (described as medium, supported by income meeting expenditure with savings and pension funds). The report does not explicitly assess whether any vulnerability or low capability characteristic materially affects the suitability of the recommendation itself.

    File:     The Risk Profile (P1, P10) records client responses showing: 'No understanding / knowledge' of stocks and shares, Enterprise Investment Schemes, Venture Capital Trusts, and pension planning risks. The client has invested only in cash and savings within the last 5 years, with no experience in unit trusts, stocks and shares, or pension products. The Capacity for Loss questionnaire section (P2) records 'No additional notes were recorded'. The agreed risk level (P3, P10, P14) is 5 Balanced. The Suitability Report (P14) states: 'John, you are aware that investment values can rise as well as fall, and although your experience is limited to your workplace pension arrangements, you understand that returns are not guaranteed. You confirmed that any potential underperformance would not have a detrimental impact on your day-to-day living.' The Financial Plan (P4, P21) shows current monthly cash flow deficit of £288 (income £1,430, expenditure £1,718). The fact find (P7, P12) records total monthly disposable income as £-288.00. The report also notes (P14): 'It would also be prudent to set up and register Power of Attorney for your financial affairs / health and welfare, to ensure that in the event of your incapacity, your spouse or relative could act on your behalf.' File notes (P22) document existing fund risk ratings: Aviva current risk 2, Zurich current risk 7, Standard Life current risk 4.

    Discrepancies:

      - Report asserts client capacity for loss is 'medium' but justification rests on income comfortably meeting expenditure with savings and pension funds; however, fact find documents monthly cash flow deficit of £288 per month (expenditure £1,718 exceeds income £1,430), contradicting 'comfortable' characterisation

      - Report states client confirmed underperformance 'would not have a detrimental impact on day-to-day living' despite documented monthly deficit of £288; low documented investment experience (no understanding of stocks and shares, EIS, VCT, pension planning risks) raises questions about informed confirmation

      - Report identifies no vulnerability despite client's documented zero knowledge of pension planning risks and limited investment experience (cash and savings only), yet recommends consolidation into multi-asset pension strategy with risk rating 5 (Balanced)

      - Report recommends risk rating 5 (Balanced) upgraded from questionnaire rating 4 but evidence shows client lacks knowledge base typical for Balanced investors; P3 describes Balanced investors as those 'with some experience of investments and a degree of understanding of financial matters'

      - Report does not address tension between stated objective (consolidation for mental clarity per P6: 'Wants to consolidate to help him get his head around what he has') and actual consolidation outcome (only 4 of 5 pensions consolidated; People's Pension retained)

      - Report recommends substantial pension consolidation (£110,185 transferred to new Aviva platform) but financial plan shows current shortfall in monthly cash flow, without analysis of whether liquidity or access implications have been reconsidered

    The core tension is between documented low financial capability and the suitability of a complex multi-asset pension consolidation. The Risk Profile (P1) and capacity questionnaire responses are unambiguous: the client demonstrates no understanding of stocks and shares, pension planning, or associated risks. The only investment experience recorded is cash and savings accounts. Yet the suitability report upgrades the client from risk rating 4 (questionnaire result) to risk rating 5 (Balanced), and recommends consolidating £110,185 into a multi-asset strategy on the new Aviva platform. P3 sets out that Balanced risk investors should have 'some experience of investments and a degree of understanding of financial matters'; this client demonstrably has neither. The report's assertion that underperformance would not impact day-to-day living is contradicted by the fact find, which shows a monthly household deficit of £288 (P12). While the report notes income and expenditure separately and cites state pension coverage of bills in retirement, the current shortfall indicates material vulnerability to cash flow disruption. The report does identify this risk in part (medium capacity for loss) but does not reconcile it with the recommendation of a consolidated, growth-oriented portfolio requiring a 10-year investment horizon. The report references Power of Attorney as prudent but does not assess whether the client's limited understanding of pension mechanics creates a dependency on ongoing adviser support or decision-making capacity that should constrain the recommendation. The six foreseeable harms documented in the canonical model are identified and mitigations are stated, but none of those mitigations directly address the client's demonstrated lack of knowledge about pension planning itself or the specific risks of switching into a consolidation vehicle the client may not fully comprehend. The report asserts consistency ('You are aware...you understand...you confirmed...') but the qualitative evidence suggests the client's confirmation may not rest on genuine comprehension. The fact that this is a low-capability profile combined with a current cash flow deficit and complex product consolidation creates material suitability risk that is not explicitly analysed in the recommendation itself.

  [G10.5] Vulnerabilities recorded in the file and in the report

  Potential Concern — severity High — citations partly verified

    Report:   The report asserts /vulnerability/perClient[]/hasVulnerability = 'No', indicating no vulnerabilities were identified or recorded in relation to the client.

    File:     The case file contains extensive evidence of characteristics and support needs that may constitute vulnerability indicators under FG21/1: (1) Client age 69 with state pension reliance at £230/week and volatile income as HGV driver at £300/week net (P1); (2) Limited investment experience confined to workplace pension arrangements (P1); (3) Negative monthly cashflow of -£288 per month with total expenditure £1,718 exceeding net income £1,430 (P9); (4) Modest liquid savings of only £6,000 against pension assets of £116,997 (P8, P19); (5) Capacity for loss assessment describes potential underperformance would not have 'detrimental impact on day-to-day living' but this sits against cashflow deficit (P1); (6) Risk rating was initially 4 per questionnaire but upgraded to 5, with notation that adviser 'felt that this didn't fully reflect your risk appetite' suggesting initial risk assessment may not have captured true position (P1); (7) Client works as HGV driver with income described as 'typically' £300/week, indicating variability not reflected in deterministic cashflow (P1, P14); (8) Fact Find shows 'No Requirement' for accessible format requirement (P10) and no additional notes recorded on investor experience or capacity for loss questionnaire (P13); (9) File notes indicate extensive alternative option analysis and fund substitution decisions (P5, P6), suggesting complexity requiring explanation.

    Discrepancies:

      - Report asserts no vulnerability (hasVulnerability='No') while file records multiple financial and demographic characteristics consistent with vulnerability indicators under FG21/1: age 69, low financial resilience (negative cashflow), limited investment experience, modest liquid assets relative to pension holdings, and volatile employment income.

      - Risk rating was upgraded from questionnaire result of 4 to agreed rating of 5, with adviser statement that initial rating 'didn't fully reflect your risk appetite', suggesting potential misalignment between initial assessment and final position that may indicate vulnerability not fully captured.

      - Cashflow shows -£288 monthly deficit (expenditure £1,718 vs income £1,430) while capacity for loss assessment states underperformance would not have 'detrimental impact on day-to-day living', presenting apparent tension with actual financial position.

      - Client income described as 'typically earning around £300 per week net' (word 'typically' indicating variability) but cashflow model appears to use deterministic £300/week assumption without qualification for income volatility.

      - Fact Find records 'No additional notes were recorded' on investor experience and capacity for loss questionnaire (P13), indicating no narrative capture of client circumstances, yet file notes contain extensive commentary on alternatives analysis and fund considerations.

    The report's assertion that hasVulnerability='No' cannot be reconciled with the evidence. The distinction between reporting hasVulnerability='No' and the absence of vulnerability data extraction must be addressed: the canonical model explicitly states the value is 'No', not absent. This is a substantive assertion that contradicts multiple indicators in the file. Under FCA FG21/1, vulnerability drivers include low financial resilience, limited capability, life events, and health/capability concerns. The file evidences: (1) Age 69 with imminent retirement decision; (2) Financial position showing negative monthly cashflow of -£288 despite capacity for loss assessment; (3) Limited investment experience restricted to workplace pensions; (4) Volatile employment income as HGV driver; (5) Modest liquid reserves (£6,000) against pension consolidation of £116,997; (6) Risk rating revision from questionnaire result of 4 to agreed 5, with adviser noting initial rating did not reflect risk appetite—this revision process itself suggests initial assessment captured misalignment. The file notes contain no evidence of vulnerability assessment being explicitly performed and rejected; instead, there is silence in the formal assessment sections (P13 records no notes) combined with operational handling of complexity (extensive alternatives analysis, fund selection complexity). The guards permit that a vulnerability client asked not to record may still have been handled correctly, and that historic vulnerabilities may not apply. However, there is no evidence the client requested non-recording of any vulnerability. The cashflow deficit and modest liquid resources are current-state financial facts recorded in the Fact Find dated 19/09/2025, not historical. The lack of any recorded discussion of vulnerability assessment—neither affirming one occurred and found none, nor documenting why characteristics were not treated as vulnerability—represents a gap in the requirement that vulnerabilities be assessed and, if found, reflected consistently throughout the report. The revision of risk rating suggests the adviser recognised initial assessment inadequacy, yet this recognition does not appear in any formal vulnerability assessment narrative. The requirement asks whether report information is 'fully supported by the evidence provided'. The report's 'No' is not supported; it is contradicted by multiple financial and demographic characteristics in the evidence.
